Full diagnosis+repro+fix-directions: DETAILS#t-win-50021-2b-crash-fixed-crt-split-remains-2026-08-20. - FINDING (win-x64, complete root-cause of the last 2 win exec reds `exec/alignas` + `exec/errors_and_warnings`; for the T-mac-30038 owner): these are FLEET-WIDE, NOT win-specific. The default `cversion` is **C23 (202311)** at `libmcc.c:1197` (single, non-platform-conditional); because C23 >= C99, `mcc_apply_std_permerrors` (`libmcc.c:844-856`, `removed_in_c99[]` set) promotes `warn_implicit_int` to a HARD error on every platform. So `_Alignas(16) i3;` (implicit-int, alignas.c:4) COMPILE-FAILS by default -> `exec/alignas` (a run test, golden `"alignas.c:4: warning: type defaults to int\n1 1 1 1\n"`) can't run -> `expect_win32` cannot fix a compile failure. Compounding: the diag message DRIFTED — golden `type defaults to int` (no quotes) vs the 4 current sites all emitting `type defaults to 'int'` (quoted): `mccgen.c:18838` "in declaration", `:18927` "return type", `:18951` bare, `:19076-19080` (the conditional `mcc_error_noabort` when `warn_implicit_int & WARN_ON` else warning — the "downgrades not suppresses" bug). The fix is a DESIGN call (compile these tests with a permissive/`-std` mode so implicit-int is a warning, OR update the tests to not rely on implicit-int compiling, PLUS reconcile the 4 message sites) + cross-platform re-verify — a T-mac-30038 (diag-owner) task, not a win-session golden bless. This closes my investigation of every win red: 5 cleared, these 2 fully root-caused and handed off.

So the recovery set is tiny: keep narrow on 99.967%, guard+restore 11 chains → 100% correct at ~full narrow speed. `needs64.c` (0% accurate) is 3-clean/1-dirty — misses isolate to 1 of 4 chains. **KEY FINDING (why this is the RIGHT next slice, not VLAT hard-promote):** the VLAT-proven hard-promote (slice 2, 1a5fc8dcb) fires ~never because provable narrows overlap the static narrow pass; the value is entirely in the SHADOW-COMPARED unprovable narrows — which a runtime boundary guard makes cheap. **SYMBIOTIC INTEGRATION MAP (investigation 2026-08-21 — the machinery already exists; net-new is small):** (a) runtime deopt store = the near-match CORRECTION TABLE — `mccjit_corr_find`/`mccjit_corr_insert` + `mccjit_nearmatch_miss` (`src/mccjit_embed.c`, MccjitKgc `corr`/`corr_n`, cap `MCCJIT_NEARMATCH_CORR_MAX`=64) is ALREADY a per-input-tuple "keep fast variant, patch divergent inputs" system; route boundary deopts into it (overflow ⇒ store baseline value for that tuple), poison only when >64 divergent tuples. (b) static prove-away = `ast_vlat_in_type`/`ast_vlat_narrowing` (`src/mccast.c`) — emit the runtime guard ONLY when VLAT is inconclusive (the `mccjit_ast_blind_retype` return-1 unproven case); return-2 stays the guard-free hard-promote. (c) narrow/restore mechanics = `ast_narrow_make` class 1/2/3 (`src/mccast.c`, reinterpret vs insert-Convert); "restore wide" = drop the narrow Convert on the sliced chain. (d) branchless clamp codegen = `gen_cmov` (`src/arch/x86_64/x86_64-gen.c`) + the INT_MAX/INT_MIN materialization pattern already in the float→int fold (`src/mccgen.c`); NO SSE saturating-pack (pmaxsd/packssdw) exists — cmov/select is the intended primitive. This is the concrete promotion mechanism the remaining work implements on top of the shipped shadow-compare slice. **SLICE 2 DONE (2026-08-21, 1a5fc8dcb): VLAT gate wired** — blind+VLAT-proven → bare trampoline (baseline skipped); blind+unproven → KGC shadow-compare; `--stats` reports proven/hard-promoted/shadow split; parity 14/14, byte-identical. **KEY FINDING:** the LLONG→INT proven-narrow OVERLAPS the existing static narrow pass (VLAT-provable narrowings are already narrowed pre-JIT; mutated accumulators aren't readonly-provable) → proven path fires ~never on real code, so the retype superopt's unique value is the SHADOW-COMPARED variants static passes can't prove (slice 1, already shipped), not the static narrow. Regression-guarded by ast/vecpacked-typecov (0177d844). So the SSE2 SINGLE-INSTRUCTION packed vector SURFACE that cleanly matches gcc is COMPLETE (float/double/int arith incl short-mul + all compares). int×4 `*` via the SSE2 pmuludq sequence DONE (T-lin-10425-part, b37d05d4: x86_64_vec16_imul32 = movaps/psrlq/pmuludq×2/pshufd×2/punpckldq; the group-opcode psrlq REX follows the existing verified pattern; mcc uses only xmm0-7 so the high-reg REX path is unexercised-but-correct; verified ==gcc incl a 7-mul pressure fn). So the ENTIRE default-march (SSE2) packed vector ARITHMETIC + COMPARISON surface that matches gcc is COMPLETE — float/double/int/short arith (all ops incl mul) + all float/int compares, regression-guarded by ast/vecpacked-typecov. (byte×16 * has no SSE2 op — gcc scalarizes, mcc matches.) ASSEMBLER self-consistency DONE (e2d3d3a2 + af71ccb0): added the SSE ops the vector codegen emits that x86_64-asm.h lacked — pmuludq, mulpd/subpd/divpd/addpd, cmpps/cmppd (imm8), pshufd (imm8) — each verified BYTE-IDENTICAL to gas; mcc's assembler now parses every SSE op its own codegen generates. -S DISASSEMBLER self-consistency DONE (6d8739c4): added decode cases for the integer/compare/shuffle SSE ops (padd/psub[bwdq], pand/por, pmullw/pmuludq/pmaddwd, punpckldq, pcmpeq/pcmpgt[bwd], cmpps/cmppd imm-predicate, pshufd imm) — mcc -S now decodes every vector op it emits, matching objdump. So the FULL emit→assemble→disassemble self-consistency for the vector surface is COMPLETE. REMAINING optimizer pieces are genuinely BLOCKED/architectural, none a bounded increment: (a) `_mm_rsqrt_ps`/rcpps — BLOCKED: no GNU-vector operator triggers it AND mcc has NO __builtin_ia32_* surface at all (verified: implicit-decl error), so it needs a whole SSE-intrinsic builtin family (token+dispatch+typecheck+codegen) + <xmmintrin.h>; also the ~2x nbody8i value is in AUTO-VECTORIZING scalar 1/sqrt (SLP), which the intrinsic alone does NOT provide; (c) FMA (vfmadd a*b+c under FP_CONTRACT) — different verification class (NOT bit-identical to -O0; verify ==gcc which also fuses); (d) the XMM-RESIDENT value class (keep vectors in regs not memory-backed — the ~2x nbody8i unlock, architectural) + SLP. - [ ] T-lin-10443 [S] `-fno-<strategy>` AST-optimizer flags are structurally INERT — most strategies (bfold/ident/cprop/cse/licm/dse/sccp/jt/tco/cload, ast_strategies[] mccast.c:17996) are gated by `sg_templates`(ast_templates_env), not their own MCC_OPT flag, and `do_<pass>` is driven by strategy fire-counts not the flags. So -fno-tree-dse/-fno-gcse/-fno-tree-loop-im/etc. do nothing (verified dse=1 with/without); flagsweep passes vacuously (only checks parse). Give each strategy a per-flag gate + make flagsweep assert a flag takes EFFECT (count drops). Medium-risk, o0-neutral (default all-on). NOT urgent (debug/usability gap, not a miscompile). SCOUT (2026-08-20): the wiring is PARTIAL + NON-UNIFORM, so it's a careful per-pass job not a one-liner. The `mcc_opt(s1, MCC_OPT_X)` query already feeds env vars at mccast.c:2546-2614 (ast_dse_call_env=MCC_OPT_TREE_DSE, ast_cse_comm_env=GCSE, ast_reassoc_env=TREE_REASSOC, ast_cse_join_env=GCSE_JOIN, ast_licm_temp_env=TREE_LOOP_IM, ast_pre_env=TREE_PRE) — some (pre/reassoc) may already gate via their env var, but the main do_<pass> block (mccast.c:21525-21541, `do_dse=dses>0` etc.) does NOT AND with any flag. Fix = AND each do_<pass> with mcc_opt(s1, MCC_OPT_<row>) using the map: do_dse↔TREE_DSE, do_cse↔GCSE, do_licm↔TREE_LOOP_IM, do_narrow↔NARROW, do_sethi↔SETHI_ULLMAN, do_cprop↔TREE_COPY_PROP (verify each; some do_<pass> have no dedicated row). CRUX BLOCKER (2026-08-20, why it's NOT a one-liner): a naive `do_dse &= mcc_opt(s1, MCC_OPT_TREE_DSE)` MISCOMPILES — TREE_DSE is LEVEL(4), so mcc_opt returns 0 at -O2/-O3, but the opt-SEARCH mechanism legitimately runs DSE there via sf[AST_STRAT_DSE] counts (do_dse = dses>0, independent of the level gate). ANDing with the level-based mcc_opt would DISABLE search-driven DSE at -O2/-O3 ⇒ codegen change, not neutral. The -fno override and the level/search enablement are ENTANGLED in mcc_opt(). Correct fix needs a query for "user EXPLICITLY passed -fno-X" that is SEPARATE from the level default (add an explicit-set bitmask at flag-parse time, gate do_<pass> only on that) — a small design change to the flag layer, not just the opt loop. Then default (nothing -fno'd) is truly byte-identical and search still works. THIS is the actual task. PARTIAL LANDED (2026-08-20, daa059d1): implemented exactly that — ast_opt_user_off[] snapshots optflag[id]==0 (the explicit -fno) at ast_opt_defaults() entry before UNSET→default resolution (optflag is memset to MCC_OPT_UNSET=0xff, so only -fno sets 0); ast_opt_user_disabled(id) queries it; gated do_dse/do_cse(gcse)/do_licm(tree-loop-im)/do_narrow/do_cprop/do_sethi on !disabled. Default byte-identical (o0-baseline + rir-parity all targets + typecov + exec 82/82). -fno-tree-loop-im (ltemp 4→0, codegen differs) + -fno-gcse (codegen differs) now genuinely control their strategy — the measurement lever the algorithm-phase work needs. FINDING: the AST strategy taxonomy heavily OVERLAPS — -fno-tree-dse/-fno-narrow/-fno-tree-copy-prop/-fno-sethi-ullman gate their strategy correctly but show no visible codegen effect because unread/sccp/the ALWAYS-on foundational passes (ident/bfold/narrow-class) do the same work; fully disabling e.g. "DSE" would need -fno on every dead-store-doing pass. REMAINING: (a) a flagsweep cell that asserts a flag takes EFFECT (codegen differs, not just parses) for the 2 verified strategies; (b) extend gating to more do_<pass> if wanted; (c) decide whether the overlapping-strategy flags should map many→one. Downgraded from the original "structurally inert" — the mechanism now works.

- [ ] T-mac-30121 [S] Fix: [MED] `__attribute__((cleanup))` jump-constraint gaps — (1) computed `goto *p` out of a cleanup scope silently SKIPS the handler → leak, no diag (`mccgen.c:16306-16311` calls ggoto() w/o cleanup analysis, unlike labeled `:16324-16330`/backward `:16335`); (2) a jump BYPASSING a cleanup var's initializer is undiagnosed → handler fires on indeterminate value/wild-free (no cleanup analog of the VLA jump guard `:16290-16302/16333-16334/16375-16376`). gcc/clang hard-error both. cleanup otherwise fully correct for valid code.       OWNER: — | STATE: OPEN | SHA: 08d945ce | TS: 2026-08-18T06:05Z
      REF: INVESTIGATIONS.md#r21-cleanup-jumps | DEPS: — | NOTE (win-x64 2026-08-19T23:58Z — sub-item (2) is a NON-BUG on current oracles, saves the dead-end): the premise "gcc/clang hard-error both" is FALSE for gcc. Verified on the win box (`goto skip; int x __attribute__((cleanup(cl)))=1; skip:;`): gcc ACCEPTS at default, `-Wall -Wextra`, and `-std=c23` (exit 0, silent) — it warns ONLY under the explicit opt-in `-Wjump-misses-init` ("jump skips variable initialization"), and even then it is a WARNING, not an error, and NOT cleanup-specific (fires on any jump-skips-init). clang is the strict outlier (errors by default: "cannot jump from this goto statement to its label"). mcc ACCEPTS → matches gcc's default, so mcc is already gcc-correct; adding clang's hard-error would DIVERGE mcc from gcc (cf. [[T-mac-30060]] / the match-gcc-not-clang rule). Do NOT implement (2) as a reject. mcc's VLA jump-guard by contrast IS correct+firing ("goto jumps into the scope of a variably modified declaration"). The only real residual is sub-item (1): computed `goto *p` out of a cleanup scope does not RUN the handler (an actual leak) — a codegen fix (run cleanup on the indirect-goto edge), not a diagnostic.

Heap kernel w/o prior `mcc_gpu_mem()` → Vulkan reads zeros, Metal faults/garbage (below the def/poison oracle). Latent behind host protocol. Fix: Metal dispatch `mtl_bind_mem(DEFAULT)`+bind buffer 2 when pipeline needs mem.

- [ ] T-mac-30253 [S] Fix: [MED] arm64-Darwin: 2+ CONSECUTIVE 1-byte (char) scalars on the stack still mismatch clang cross-module — the stack-arg encoding `a[i]` reserves its low bit as the by-ref marker (`gfunc_prolog` decodes `((a[i]-32)>>1<<1)`, passes `a[i]&1`; `arm64-gen.c`), so T-mac-30080 rounds named scalar stack offsets to min-align-2 and a 2nd adjacent char that Apple places at an odd offset lands 1 byte high. Every non-adjacent-char case is exact. Fix needs widening the `a[]`/by-ref encoding so odd stack offsets are representable, then pack char to align 1. Repro: `adj(a1..a8,char,char,char,int)` → 5233 not 6543 (mcc caller ↔ clang callee). Split from T-mac-30080 (DETAILS#t-mac-30080-arm64-macho-stack-pack).

The named/variadic boundary in `arm64_pcs_aux` and the va_list gr/vr save-area layout (`gfunc_prolog`) must be reconciled so named stack args before `...` pack to natural size/align while variadic args stay 8-slotted. - [ ] T-mac-30071 [S] Fix: `-S` textual asm emitter (disassembles the object to ELF GAS text; self-reassembly of strings/structs/GOT/float/jump-tables ROBUST) — [HIGH] `-S`→reassemble downgrades `__thread` `STT_TLS`→`STT_NOTYPE` (`emit_sym_decl` `mccdis.c:187-198` has no TLS case) → TLS relocs resolved vs a non-TLS symbol → SIGBUS; BREAKS mcc's OWN `mcc -S | mcc -c` loop (`__thread int tv; return tv;` → 138); fix emit `.type name,@tls_object`; [HIGH] arm64 intra-section `b`/`bl` emitted as bare hex `b 0x84 //.L84` (`arm64-dis.c:218-225`) → GAS/LLVM read PC-relative → wrong control flow (mcc-self OK, x86_64 correct via `.L` label); fix emit `b .L<t>`; [MED] `.align N` (byte count) read as `2^N` by arm64 GAS → 256× over-align (`mccdis.c:410`; fix `.balign`/`.p2align`); [LOW] arm64 `b.cc`/`cbz`/`tbz`/`cset` flood `.long`. RECONFIRMED Round 34 (#r34-robust): TLS `-S` self-loop still broken, symptom now SIGSEGV (was SIGBUS) — the disassembler renders the TLS-relocated access as a plain `adrp/add #:lo12:` (dropping the thread-local reloc) + places vars in `.tdata`/`.tbss`, so on reassembly `_tl` resolves as a non-TLS static address → fault. ALSO: mcc's `-S` output is NOT consumable by clang/native `as` at all — emits ELF/GNU-as directives on Darwin (`.type name,@function`/`@object` `mccdis.c:195-197`, `.section …,@progbits`/`@nobits` `:396-406`, `.size` `:537`, `.note.GNU-stack` `:624`, `.align` byte-count `:410`) → `clang -c mcc.s` errors "unknown directive .type … @function". mcc's `.s` is only consumable by mcc's own internal assembler (self-loop OK for non-TLS). Fix (portability): gate the emitter to Mach-O/`as`-compatible directives on Darwin, or document `-S` as mcc-internal-only. KEY: the Round-33 `.word`/`.align` assembler bugs do NOT bite mcc's own `-S` (uses `.byte` runs + self-consistent `.align`).

      REF: INVESTIGATIONS.md#r8-builtins | DEPS: — | NOTE (mac-arm64 2026-08-20): the [MED] glibc-only-builtin link-fail sub-item DONE (213d13e2) — mempcpy already fixed (T-mac-30170); `stpncpy` is a NON-ISSUE (in macOS libc, mcc==gcc==clang rc0); the 3 `*_unlocked` (printf/fprintf/fputs_unlocked) now provided inline under __APPLE__||_WIN32 atop locked vprintf/vfprintf/fputs (unlocked=optimization, locked always correct) — was a hard link-fail (`unresolved _printf_unlocked`); gcc-16 ALSO fails there (lowers to other missing _unlocked syms), clang lacks the builtins, so this EXCEEDS the local oracles. Linux #else byte-identical, treegate+corpusgate green, TDD cli/unlocked_stdio_builtins. REMAINING: [MED] non-const clz/ctz/ffs -> runtime call on non-x86 (perf) + [LOW] overflow-builtin const-fold/_BitInt(N>64). | INVESTIGATION sub-item (2) non-const clz/ctz->runtime-call on arm64 (mac-arm64 2026-08-21): verified — mcc emits `U ___builtin_clz`/`___builtin_ctz` calls (mccrt provides them) on arm64. DISPATCH: mccgen.c ~14883 has `#if MCC_TARGET_X86_64 { if((BB_CLZ||BB_CTZ)&&bitscan_inline_on()) gen_bitscan(...) }` (x86 bsr only); the popcount/parity/clrsb branch uses gen_bit_builtin (mccgen.c:890) which does NOT handle CLZ/CTZ (only emit_popcount_expr-based); so on arm64 CLZ/CTZ fall through to vpush_helper_func+gfunc_call. TWO fix options: (a) IDEAL — add a native arm64 `clz` (and `rbit;clz` for ctz) emitter primitive in arm64-gen.c + route CLZ/CTZ there (matches gcc's single-instr, needs backend+RIR-capture work like gen_bitscan); (b) software — extend gen_bit_builtin with `clz(x)=W-popcount(smear_right(x))` / `ctz(x)=popcount(~x&(x-1))` reusing emit_popcount_expr (inline, no call/dep, but multi-instr, DIVERGES from gcc's 1 clz). Either DRIFTS the arm64 + arm64-osx o0-baseline -> needs a native arm64-osx re-bank (doable on this box). Correctness: clz(0) is UB (match the runtime helper's current 32/64), ctz via rbit+clz. NOT a clean gen-hook — real backend/codegen task, deferred (perf+dep, not a correctness bug; current call-path is correct). REFINEMENT (2026-08-21): (i) CORRECTNESS is well-covered — tests/exec/codegen/bitscan_inline.c is a NON-arch-gated RUNTIME check (clz/ctz vs a reference over random values, golden "clz 31 0 63 / ctz 0 31 40 / OK"), so any correct impl passes regardless of inline-vs-call. (ii) The REAL non-triviality is RIR-capture: gen_bit_builtin is invoked via `ir_cap_gen_bit_builtin` (mccgen.c:199/105), so adding BB_CLZ/BB_CTZ ops must be made AST/RIR-replay-safe (the T-mac-30246 ast_locrec/replay hazard) — not just a dispatch tweak. (iii) Blast radius CAN be contained: gate the new inline `#elif MCC_TARGET_ARM64` (like the x86 gen_bitscan block) so only arm64 drifts -> arm64-osx o0-rebank is mac-native-doable, no cross-fleet coordination. Software math (reuse CLRSB's smear+emit_popcount_expr): clz=bw-popcount(x|=x>>1..); ctz=popcount((x-1)&~x). Native arm64 `clz`/`rbit;clz` is the gcc-matching ideal but needs a new arm64-gen.c primitive. Deferred: RIR-capture + rebank, a dedicated codegen session. READY-TO-EXECUTE (2026-08-21, corrects the RIR pessimism above): the SOFTWARE path is RIR-SAFE — it decomposes into existing primitive gen_op calls exactly like gen_bit_builtin's CLRSB case (mccgen.c:891-907: gv_dup/gen_op SHR/'|'/emit_popcount_expr/gen_op '-'), each already replay-captured, so NO new opaque op / no T-mac-30246 hazard (that only bites the native-`clz`-instruction path). RECIPE: add to gen_bit_builtin — BB_CLZ: `for(sh=1;sh<bw;sh<<=1){gv_dup;vpushi(sh);gen_op(TOK_SHR);gen_op('|');} emit_popcount_expr(bw); vpushi(bw);vswap();gen_op('-');`  BB_CTZ: `gv_dup;vpushi(1);gen_op('-');vswap;vpushi(-1);gen_op('^');gen_op('&'); emit_popcount_expr(bw);` then at the ~14883 dispatch add `#elif MCC_TARGET_ARM64` routing CLZ/CTZ to it. O0-REBANK IS REQUIRED: the o0_ab corpus is `find tests/exec -name '*.c'` (tools/o0_ab.sh:262) which INCLUDES tests/exec/codegen/bitscan_inline.c + tests/exec/features_c99_c11/builtin_bitops_fold.c (both use clz/ctz) -> arm64-osx sha drifts; arm64-gating keeps it to that ONE mac-native-rebankable key (see the o0_ab BANK cmds in [[moderncc-coordination-protocol]]). Correctness auto-covered by the non-gated runtime golden bitscan_inline.c ("clz 31 0 63 / ctz 0 31 40 / OK"). This is now a clean self-contained mac codegen slice for a fresh session. CODE VERIFIED + REVERTED (2026-08-21) — I implemented the exact recipe above and it WORKS: arm64 clz/ctz emit INLINE (no `U ___builtin_clz`/ctz), all 47 clz/ctz tests pass incl the bitscan_inline runtime golden AND every RIR-replay/promote/narrowfix/O1/O2/O3/Os/diff3 variant (confirms the software-primitive path IS RIR-safe). BUT reverted — CORRECTS my "arm64-gating -> one mac-rebankable key" claim: keys.txt (tests/ast/o0-baseline/keys.txt) shows `#if MCC_TARGET_ARM64` drifts TWO ACTIVE o0 keys — `arm64-osx` (mac-native rebank) AND `arm64-win32` (measured by the LINUX cross-build, NOT mac) — PLUS rir-coverage-census. So landing it is a FLEET-COORDINATED rebank (mac banks arm64-osx+rir-coverage, lin banks arm64-win32), reddening lin's gate until they act — not a solo mac slice, and not worth imposing for a moderate perf win (call-path is correct) at a marathon tail. TO LAND (coordinated session): re-apply the recipe, `O0_AB_BANK=1` arm64-osx on mac, CONTRACT lin to rebank arm64-win32 + rir-coverage-census. Code is proven; only the cross-fleet rebank coordination remains.

So it's build-host-dependent nondeterminism / latent UB (uninit read, address-order iteration, or address-value-dependent branch) in arena replay, NOT a tooling or banking fix (both shortcuts human-banned). VERDICT: deep/multi-session for the FIX, but a BOUNDED first slice exists — CONCRETE NEXT STEP: (1) confirm selfhost-fixpoint green; (2) build a self-hosted (stage-1, mcc-compiled) mcc via tools/selfhost-fixpoint.py's compile_mcc/link_mcc recipe (L113-122); (3) capture per-body TSV `MCC_RIR_PROD=2 <mcc> -c src/mcc.c` with the gcc-built mcc (cmake-debug/mcc) vs the self-hosted mcc; (4) diff the two row sets to NAME the ~2 bodies whose verdict flips + their `why` (len/bytes/rellen/relcontent); (5) instrument ast_replay_body for those bodies and make the replay a pure function of input. rir-coverage.py has NO explicit-mcc override (find_mcc uses <build>/mcc), so step 3 needs the self-hosted mcc placed as <build>/mcc or the census invoked directly. - [ ] T-lin-10020 [S] i386 `R_386_TLS_GOTIE` gap, and the declined upstream `7f7845cd` (VT_VOID)
      OWNER: — | STATE: OPEN | SHA: 1695806f | TS: 2026-08-14T12:40Z
      REF: DETAILS.md#t-lin-10020-i386-r-386-tls-gotie-gap | DEPS: — | NOTE: REPRODUCER FOUND (win-x64 via WSL, NOT claimed) — the gap is a REAL correctness bug, not just a missed optimization. `extern __thread int x; return x;` non-PIC: mcc-i386 emits R_386_TLS_LE (assumes exe-local static TLS), gcc -m32 emits R_386_TLS_GOTIE (initial-exec) — mcc's LE is WRONG for a thread-local defined in a shared lib. `-fPIC` path agrees (both GD). mcc has no IE model (gen_tls_addr only does GD/LDM; non-PIC leaf shortcuts to LE at i386-gen.c:236). FIX (needs a sysroot-equipped Linux session): emit IE/GOTIE for extern non-PIC TLS in i386-gen.c + add R_386_TLS_IE/GOTIE to i386-link.c (has GD/LDM/LDO_32/LE only) + verify under qemu-i386 with a DSO-defined __thread. BLOCKER here: no i386 sysroot / qemu-i386 on this box (T-lin-10388 provisioning gap). Full compile-level repro + fix scope: DETAILS.md#t-lin-10020-i386-r-386-tls-gotie-gap (Investigation 2026-08-15)
- [ ] T-lin-10023 [S] `-O3` re-emission leaves the pre-inline body in `.text`, and only deferral can reclaim it
      OWNER: — | STATE: OPEN | SHA: 5b166f50 | TS: 2026-08-17T15:20Z
      REF: DETAILS.md#t-lin-10023-o3-re-emission-leaves-the-pre | DEPS: —
      NOTE (mac-arm64, 2026-08-17, investigation banked; released un-shipped): traced the full implementation surface. DESIGN (confirmed sound): the orphan is the original body of a keep_reemit function (ast_reemit_retain, src/mccast.c:3772 — retained when inlining is on AND the fn has a static call); at end-of-TU ast_reemit_forward_inlines (21946) re-emits the forward-inlined ones grafted via ast_reemit (21838), leaving the original as orphan. Fix = rewind the body at retain time in ast_func_end (it's the tail of .text/.rela.text/.eh_frame there) + re-emit at end-of-TU. IMPLEMENTATION SURFACE mapped: (a) ast_func_begin (16800) saves ast_body_ind_sv + ast_reloc0_sv at body start but NO eh_frame/debug offset — must add an ast_ehframe0_sv (and any .debug_line/.debug_info start) capture; (b) the existing rewind idiom (20869-20872) truncates .text (ind=ast_body_ind_sv) + .rela.text (ast_rsec->data_offset=ast_reloc0_sv) but NOT .eh_frame — the deferral must extend it to truncate the per-fn FDE back to the saved eh_frame offset (+ debug); this NEW multi-section truncation is the crux and the silent-catastrophic-failure surface; (c) HARD CONSTRAINT: once rewound, EVERY retained fn must be re-emitted (currently only ast_reemit_has_forward ones are — the rest keep their in-place body); so ast_reemit_forward_inlines must re-emit ALL pool entries or non-forward-inlined retained fns silently VANISH. BLAST RADIUS: only -O1+ (retention is off at -O0, so o0-baseline is untouched); all -O1+ retained bodies move to end-of-TU → emitmap/selfhost/o3 baselines re-bank across 7 targets. VERIFICATION per DoD: ast.orphan_bytes/orphan_fn under MCC_INV=1 on -O3 self-compile → ~0, ast/reemit-cfi green (covers re-emitted FDEs), ^exec 8023/8023. Not shipped from mac this session: it's a marginal (~4.3% of .text) SIZE optimization whose risky core is new multi-section (.eh_frame/.debug) truncation with a silent-unwind-corruption failure mode on the SHARED compiler — wants a careful focused pass (deep-emission-pipeline context) with the full verification loop, not a rushed slower-box change. The map above de-risks that pass.

- [ ] T-lin-10074 [S] `slice/quiesce` is structurally flaky and the device lock is built, priced and off
      OWNER: — | STATE: OPEN | SHA: 1695806f | TS: 2026-08-20T14:30Z
      REF: DETAILS.md#t-lin-10074-slicequiesce-is-structurally-flaky-and-the | DEPS: — | NOTE (lin-x64, investigated, released on user pivot to optimizer work): the `-DMCC_DEVICE_LOCK` mechanism is a ctest RESOURCE_LOCK (CMakeLists.txt:10936, off) — TEST-harness only, touches no mcc runtime. Root of the flake is cross-process contention on the SHARED physical GPU (separate ctest processes each own a device; `slice/quiesce`'s teardown under -jN load makes peers' submit+wait time out). Per the "mcc must cope with load" directive the fix is mcc-runtime: a cross-process advisory `flock` folded into `MCC_GPU_LOCK`/`MCC_GPU_UNLOCK` (src/mccgpu.c:32-33) — default-ON, auto (instant when uncontended, serializes only under real multi-process contention), `flock` auto-releases on process death (no stale-lock). Env off-switch `MCC_GPU_XLOCK=0`. Needs sys/file.h+fcntl.h includes + xlock helpers. Ties to T-lin-10081 (narrow the in-proc lock via per-context resident state, mccgpu.c:1837) + budget auto-modes (`--jit-gpu-budget=auto` is stubbed unimplemented, mcc.c:103; CPU `auto`=-2 idle-core path works, libmcc.c:3596). Flake did NOT reproduce in 3×26-cell -j8 runs this session (rare).
